**Ordered work (each step names its owner; nothing new until the owner is checked):**
1. Prove one packet through `hyperragPacketRpc` → packet control word → MessagePack codec → back, with identity unchanged.
2. Reuse existing ATLASBIT and ordinal-selection regressions. Add only the missing integration proof that decodes actual driver-returned PostgreSQL `bytea` using explicit `BYTEA_PAYLOAD` framing and binds it to the same frozen ordinal-map digest; fixture-only varlena simulation is insufficient.
3. `ComputeDescriptorV1` adapter and `search-runtime-compute-bridge-v1.ts` wrapper bind packetKey to matching map/snapshot/ATLASBIT/Top-K identities, apply the bitmap before SearchRuntime scoring, and emit descriptors only after replay verification and ContextManifest admission. The descriptor binds `inputArtifactRef` and digest to the existing TensorArtifactManifestV1 schema with explicit `MANIFEST_ONLY` status; it does not prove stored bytes or execute an N-API/gRPC backend. Focused adapter/owner suites pass 26/26. Trusted artifact byte resolution, production caller, PostgreSQL bytea readback, and backend resource enforcement remain unproven.
4. Run the same frozen packet/bitmap/Top-K through the CPU path and `crates/turbovec-napi`; assert identical candidate identities and declared numeric tolerance. Benchmark only the frozen cohort.
5. Emit one checksummed receipt covering validation, bitmap validation, compute, ordinal mapping and readback.
6. Only then consider the gRPC/Protobuf adapter around the same validated descriptor (round-trip parity with MessagePack for identity, version, digests, bit order and rejection).

- **Feature-label derivation contract (2026-10-24):** Treat `feature_label` as a human-readable summary of what a feature does, derived from joined feature/source/packet/AST/ontology evidence and related-file facts. Join by stable canonical feature/packet/source identities plus matching source/workspace/taxonomy revisions; retain exact evidence references for each derived claim. Oaklib may normalize candidate ontology terms/aliases, but reviewed ontology-linked tuples remain the owner of admitted concept relationships. FeatureRow numeric arrays may contain only validated numeric features with per-lane provenance; label text and ontology tuple records stay in descriptor/evidence metadata. RPC carries versioned descriptors and artifact references; bulk tensor bytes stay in Arrow/raw binary buffers, not JSON/MessagePack. **OPEN:** prove the existing multi-table source joins and authorized source scope, then test one deterministic derived label → reviewed tuple/evidence → FeatureRow → ordinal/tensor descriptor round trip. No live provider, workspace admission, tuple promotion, database write, or tensor execution is established by the schema drafts or fixture tests.

The manual label schemas are not interchangeable: `20261020_atlas_feature_labels_v1.sql` binds `feature_id` to `atlas_features`, while the unapplied `20261021_feature_identity_v2.sql` binds `canonical_feature_id` through a separately drafted concept/crosswalk owner. Both SQL files explicitly say NOT APPLIED. The RPC numeric packet intentionally carries numeric feature rows plus identity/revision/evidence arrays, not descriptive labels. ### Parent Atlas agent handoff dependency (2026-10-15)

DSPy/GEPA optimize a constrained program or routing policy; they do not own the Parent Atlas agent, retrieval, context admission, tool authorization, or durable task state. The runtime integration owner remains `parent-atlas-ace-rlm-bitfrost-integration` (`agent-execution-spine-v1.ts` for Prime/RLM contracts; ACE/ContextManifest for admitted evidence; governed-compute for deterministic DAG legality).

The production dependency order is: server-owned ContextManifest admission and readback → bounded Prime/RLM caller proof → isolated DSPy worker consuming a serialized evidence-handle request → `semantic_768` trace conversion without raw-vector duplication → pinned GEPA runtime → frozen train/validation/held-out split → shadow-only evaluation → human-reviewed promotion receipt. The Prime/RLM runtime path and offline DSPy/GEPA optimization are separate; feedback from production can become training data only after validator-backed outcome qualification and split assignment. No GEPA result may alter evidence identity, capability permissions, DAG legality, validator truth, or production prompts automatically.

Current evidence remains contract/source-level only: the execution-spine and DSPy-output specs passed 18/18 in the 2026-10-15 focused run; no live Prime caller, DSPy worker, GEPA optimizer run, qualified training cohort, or held-out evaluation was proven. Keep all checkboxes above open until their individual receipts and readbacks exist.

### RPC, tensor, and agent handoff ordering (2026-10-24 review)

- Required order: admitted source/workspace identity and evidence → canonical ontology/domain crosswalk and evidence-bound tuples → revision-qualified FeatureRow/ordinal map → EmbeddingGemma `semantic_768` and separate lexical/AST evidence → SearchRuntime single-owner fusion (PageRank only as a graph-revision-qualified feature) → admitted ContextManifest/PromptPlan → descriptor packet and optional tensor artifact → CPU tensor parity → optional LibTorch/RTX execution → agent orchestration.
- Keep `llama-server`/Ornith as synthesis over validated text/context via its HTTP/JSON API. It is not the embedding owner or tensor transport. Keep TypeScript↔DSPy control on HTTP/JSON; MCP uses JSON-RPC 2.0. Do not add gRPC/A2A merely to transport tensors or bypass evidence admission.

Reuse `embedding-tensor-artifact-v2.ts` for tensor metadata/checksum and Python `arrow_adapter.py`/Arrow IPC or a checksummed contiguous F32 artifact for bulk data and mmap. PostgreSQL `bytea` is not a substitute for a typed, revisioned artifact contract. Keep canonical `semantic_768` separate from FP16/INT8 challenger representations; each conversion needs an explicit representation revision and parity proof.
- `feature-row-rpc-packet-v1.ts` is now hardened as a staged descriptor + binary-buffer builder: explicit row-major `float32-le`, LSB0 presence bitmap (nullable `pprAffinity`/`crossEncoder` no longer collapse indistinguishably to zero), and per-row packet/canonical/source/source-revision/evidence lineage; duplicate candidate identities, length drift, metadata mismatch, and checksum mismatch refuse decoding. MessagePack carries metadata and mask descriptor, while numeric bytes remain separate. Focused tests 6/6 and file-scoped TypeScript check pass. It has no production callers and does not itself authorize/admit FeatureRows. Python in the current repo venv has CPU PyTorch but no `msgpack` package, so the TypeScript MessagePack packet has no demonstrated Python consumer; Arrow IPC remains the existing cross-language bulk-data owner. No Python/LibTorch/Arrow live round-trip is claimed.

### JEPA / GEPA-768 INPUT LINEAGE AUDIT — 2026-09-27 (read-only)

- The saved Packet-JEPA training export contains 2,979 pair rows and 197 evaluation rows; every row in both files has `input_dim=64`. The exporter chooses `content_embedding_384`/`embedding` only when its parsed vector has at least 128 elements, otherwise it falls back to `latent_64`, then keeps the dominant dimension. `content_embedding_384` is the legacy 384-dim column dropped from `codebase_chunk_index` on 2026-08-30 (archived per CLAUDE.md's Embedding Dimensions Policy, zero data loss verified at drop time) — the exporter's column choice predates that drop and is not a currently reachable source. The canonical/derived lane set today is `semantic_768` (embeddinggemma, canonical primary) plus derived `MRL-512`, `MRL-256`, `MRL-128` truncations and the separately-trained `latent_256`/`latent_128`/`latent_64` autoencoder projections (see CLAUDE.md, same section, for population counts per lane). The observed export therefore trained on 64-D vectors via a since-retired column path, not semantic_768 or any of its current derived lanes.
- The saved training report labels its cosine baseline `embedding384_cosine` while reporting `input_dim=64` — the label refers to the exporter's now-dropped `content_embedding_384` fallback logic above, not a live 384-dim retrieval lane (384 remains retired repo-wide, per CLAUDE.md; do not reintroduce it). Treat its JEPA/PCA metrics as historical and dimension/source-ambiguous; they are not evidence for a 768-D (or 512/256/128-MRL, or latent-256/128/64) comparison. No JEPA training or artifact regeneration was run in this audit.
- The saved pair/eval rows carry packet keys and vectors, but lack the `source_ref + source_revision + content_hash + representation_revision` identity tuple and immutable model/representation checksums required by `GEPA-768-INPUT-01`. The current Python bridge exposes `RepairMetricObservationV1` for scoring, but no typed semantic-retrieval-trace adapter was found.
- Code-only progress: `build_semantic_768_gepa_example_v1()` in `python/parent_atlas_dspy_repair.py` validates the stated metadata-only example shape, rejects missing/placeholder revisions, non-768 representations, lane/executor conflation, and unknown/raw-vector fields, and emits a deterministic checksum over the source/revision/content/representation identity tuple. The existing `SearchRuntime.searchWithAceManifest()` is not called by this application route, and the typed ACE production-source adapter has no production `resolveCanonicalSources` owner. A separate legacy SvelteKit path is present: `assembleACEContext()` calls `ace/query-router.routeQuery()`, consumes the returned packet's ranked cards/context, and the router reads/writes Valkey ACE packet and retrieval-cache keys; `nearestCluster()` reads cached centroid vectors and may set packet/context cluster metadata. The current `qdrantSearch()` call does not pass a cluster filter, so comments must not claim cluster-scoped ANN. The assembler attaches its legacy compiled ACE `contextManifest`; this is distinct from `ContextManifestV2`. Its optional `revisionedContextCache` path requires the caller to supply a complete V2 manifest and is not configured by this retrieval path. Thus the legacy Valkey-to-ACE assembly exists, but its cache packet/source references are not verified against current source/workspace revisions and do not establish admitted V2 evidence. Keep modern SearchRuntime/ContextManifest admission open until the canonical source owner supplies revision-qualified profiles/evidence and independent readback; do not treat cache hints as evidence or invent missing revisions. Focused `context-assembler.spec.ts` passed 10/10, but does not exercise the dynamic `routeQuery()` integration. Source inspection/test only; no runtime search or persistent store writes performed.

## Product feature decomposition runtime integration (2026-10-09)

The product-feature helper at `scripts/atlas/lib/product-feature-decomposition-v1.mjs` remains a pure JavaScript proposal producer. Its `featureLabel` is request intent, not packet-summary `feature_label`, canonical domain classification, an ontology ID, or admission. Complete its integration through existing owners rather than introducing a second query classifier, search engine, ontology registry, or DAG executor; the vocabulary contract is owned by `PFDECOMP-01..10` in `parent-atlas-ontology-kernel/tasks.md`.

- [ ] **PF-CONTEXT-01** Build ContextManifest only from exact revision-qualified source evidence and admitted typed relations, then independently read back its canonical checksum. Do not treat source-span verification or proposal output as admission.

## Canonical-state correction — 2026-10-14

- Canonical dense representation is `semantic_768` (EmbeddingGemma, 768-d). The 384 representation is retired from active retrieval; 384 references above are historical and are not current canonical claims.
- Latent/MRL facts (code is authoritative; root `CLAUDE.md` "Autoencoder latents"): `z128` is a learned bottleneck, not a slice of `z256`; `z64 = normalize(z128[:64])`; MRL prefix `v_k = normalize(v_768[:k])`. Existing `latent_128` rows described as a slice of `latent_256` must be re-verified before citation.
- Parity canonical contract is `codebase_chunks_768_v2`; `codebase_chunks_768` is legacy.
- This note changes no database, Qdrant, or runtime state.

### Decision: self-prompting owner and :8095 alignment (2026-10-24, operator)

- **SELF-PROMPT-OWNER-01 decided:** `src/lib/server/ace/self-prompt.ts` is the canonical self-prompting owner for production self-evaluation and single retry. It has live callers (`api/synthesis/generate`, `api/sse/chat`, `api/investigate/suggest`, `api/analytics/mapreduce-matrix`, and the analytics page). The DSPy worker caller is the proposal-only repair boundary: it has no production callers yet and does not do self-evaluation.
- **Alignment with `:8095`:** query analysis stays on the FastAPI NLP sidecar's `POST /analyze` (`python/miniforge_nlp_sidecar_v2.py`). The DSPy worker does not call `:8095` today. Any future DSPy use of `:8095` output must treat it as grounded evidence with source spans and must not promote it into identity.
- **ORNITH-TOOLCALL-01 completed:** Implemented `ornith-tool-call-guard-v1.ts` with strict Zod validation for model tool call invocations (`read_evidence`, `query_symbol_facts`, `get_manifest_chunk`), ContextManifest V2 tool allowlists, and RLM recursion bounds (`maxRecursionDepth: 3`, `maxToolCalls: 5`). 5/5 unit tests pass in `ornith-tool-call-guard-v1.test.ts`. Full atlas evals suite (31/31 passed).

### END-TO-END: ranked update prompts from cached procedural sequences, attached to feature_label (2026-10-24)

Status: PLANNED. Nothing below is implemented. Live check (read-only): `public.atlas_procedural_sequences_v1` is ABSENT from `legal_ai_db`. The MCP tool `atlas.procedural_sequence_search` (`src/mcp/trace-mcp-server.ts` ~line 9859) queries that table, so it has no data behind it today.

| Step | What | Owner | Status | Gate to pass |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Storage DDL | `drizzle/manual/20261018_atlas_procedural_sequences_v1_DRAFT.sql`. PK `(sequence_id, sequence_revision)`; `admission_status` in PROPOSED/REVIEWED/ADMITTED/REJECTED/RETIRED; `canonical_authority=false`; `execution_allowed=false`; `sequence_checksum` format-checked | PostgreSQL 18 via manual SQL | DRAFT, not applied | Explicit approval; migration test in a transaction ending ROLLBACK (PG18-MIG-TEST-01); pre-image note; post-check |
| 2. Drizzle schema entry | Declare the table in `src/lib/server/db/schema-postgres.ts` so `drizzle-kit` cannot plan a DROP | Drizzle ORM 0.44 | NOT_STARTED | Drizzle Safety Rule: no `drizzle-kit push`; `tablesFilter` check |
| 3. Zod step contract | Zod schema for one procedural step and one sequence. Reuse the `evalSchema` pattern in `src/lib/server/ace/self-prompt.ts`; reject `<think>`, `<|channel>`, `chainOfThought`, `hiddenThoughts` markers | Zod | NOT_STARTED | Unit tests: valid, invalid, and reasoning-marker rejection |
| 4. Candidate generation | Ornith 1.5 (`ornith-1.5-9b`) on llama-server `:8090`: `stream: true`, parse the SSE stream, validate with step 3, never persist hidden reasoning or KV | llama-server `:8090` | NOT_STARTED | Output rejected when it fails step 3; raw model output preserved on rejection |
| 5. Upsert write | Insert-only per revision: `onConflictDoNothing` on `(sequence_id, sequence_revision)`. Same key with a different checksum is an identity collision and fails closed. No silent overwrite of an admitted row | Drizzle + PostgreSQL 18 | NOT_STARTED | Readback after write; rollback test; no write without approval |
| 6. Join to feature_label | Resolve canonical feature identity through the reviewed source-identity/concept crosswalk; select the label by canonical feature ID plus label revision. Treat `feature_label` as descriptive summary text and verify it against its evidence refs, never as an identity/join key. | Existing feature identity and reviewed-label owners | BLOCKED on lineage | Reject label-only matches, unreviewed labels, stale source/workspace revisions, and ontology tuples without exact evidence bindings |
| 7. Ranking | Rank only ADMITTED rows, ordered by a score that has a defined owner. No ranking formula is invented here | SearchRuntime owns fusion (not decided) | BLOCKED on owner decision | Named ranking owner plus a receipt |
| 8. Cache | Valkey holds ranked prompts keyed by `(feature_id, sequence_revision)`. Invalidate after each PostgreSQL write. The cache is never truth | BitFrost (disposable) | NOT_STARTED | Write-after-commit order; expiry test |
| 9. Prompt assembly | Ranked prompts enter a ContextManifest, not a raw sequence dump. Citations must resolve to admitted rows | ContextManifest V2 | NOT_STARTED | Manifest checksum readback |
| 10. Eval | Held-out split of sequences frozen before optimization. Compare baseline and candidate on the same split | GEPA-HELDOUT-01 | OPEN | Frozen IDs; no promotion without review (PROMOTION-01) |

Dependencies: step 1 is the first real gate and needs explicit approval. Steps 3 and 4 can be built and tested without a database. Step 7 is blocked until a ranking owner is named.

- **2026-10-24 feature-description contract (contract-tested only):** Added `atlas.feature-description.v1` as a separate descriptive/evidence descriptor. It binds the description to the `EvidenceLocatorV1` canonical ID, packet key, source ref, source/workspace revisions, content digest, and exact evidence references; related-file refs must match their evidence identity and revision. The builder can only emit `PROPOSAL_ONLY`; only a separate human-review record may declare `REVIEWED`. A separate verifier hashes supplied source bytes and span slices and returns a checksummed byte-verification receipt, but this does not prove that the provider supplied bytes for the claimed revision. The descriptor is intentionally excluded from numeric `FeatureRowV1`, tensors, RPC payloads, ontology admission, and authorization. Focused tests cover valid binding and mismatched/dangling/tampered evidence. It has no production caller; canonical packet resolution, independent revision authority, reviewed ontology tuples, and authorized workspace admission remain separate blockers.
- **2026-10-24 FeatureRow-description join (fixture-only):** Added a wrapper that joins `FeatureRowV1` to the separate description only on canonical ID, packet key, source ref, feature/workspace/source revisions, then verifies evidence bytes/spans and emits `canonicalAuthority=false`/`writesPerformed=false`. It refuses `REVIEWED` labels unless the reviewed-label owner is connected, so callers cannot assert a review decision through this join. Its tests verify the descriptor stays out of strict FeatureRow/RPC numerics and does not change the Float32 feature vector. Eight descriptor/join tests plus the forged-review regression and 20 existing FeatureRow/bridge/bundle tests pass. `tsc --noEmit` still reports the unrelated existing `atlas-semantic-tools.ts:442` missing `AtlasSearchResponse.graphExpanded`. Existing `promoteCandidateFeatureRowV1` validates lexical, AST, ontology, semantic representation, and PageRank dimensions but has no non-test caller; this wrapper also has no production provider/caller. It does not solve missing actor authorization, authoritative workspace currentness, canonical packet resolution, or admitted-snapshot lineage.
- **2026-10-24 feature-label revision comparison (diagnostic only):** The summary ranker now reads packet `source_revision` and `workspace_revision_key` only when those columns exist and compares them with the revisions reported in summary-layer metadata. It emits explicit missing/incomplete/mismatch states; an exact metadata match is named `REVISION_METADATA_MATCH_UNVERIFIED` because matching declarations do not prove summary bytes, provenance, authority, or admission. The status does not affect ranking or write eligibility. Seven `derive-feature-identity` tests pass. No database or cache run was performed; live schema/query compatibility remains unverified.
- **2026-10-24 PostgreSQL 18.4 summary/map upsert audit (read-only):** Catalog read used `BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY`. `atlas_feature_map` has `normalized_path` primary key plus nonunique indexes on `feature_id`, `qdrant_point_id`, and `som_cluster`; fields do not include `feature_label`, `source_revision`, or `workspace_revision_key`. It has 4,748 rows, 1,992 non-null `packet_id` values, and 0 duplicate `source_ref` values, but **none of the 4,748 `packet_id` values matched `atlas_packets.packet_id` or `atlas_packets.packet_key`** in a literal read-only join. Treat map packet IDs as an unresolved identity space; do not backfill labels/revisions or use them as conflict identity. Existing map writer uses `ON CONFLICT (normalized_path) DO NOTHING`, which prevents refresh and has no revision qualification. `atlas_summary_layers` has 18,437 rows; 1,545 duplicate `(packet_key, COALESCE(layer_type, summary_level))` groups; no source/workspace revision columns, and 0 rows report either revision in metadata. It has packet-key indexes but no unique natural-key constraint, so a summary upsert target is not established. `batch-summarize-packets.mjs` directly calls Ornith-compatible llama-server and updates `atlas_packets.summary` by `packet_key`; it is not LangExtract and does not persist revision/evidence provenance. LangExtract already has an OpenAI-compatible provider configured through `LANGEXTRACT_BASE_URL` (default Docker route to llama-server `:8090`) in the NLP sidecar, and a separate exact-quote fixture probe; summary composition is not proven. BitFrost has a bounded bucket plan and executor with caller-provided reconstructors, but no production caller was found; `warmBitFrostCandidateV1` likewise has no caller. Do not warm caches or index summaries from unqualified rows. **Next:** resolve the existing packet/source identity owner and select the revisioned descriptive-label owner before proposing migration columns/unique upsert keys. No DDL/DML, cache writes, model inference, or workspace/authority changes occurred.
- **2026-10-24 correction — map packet identity owner:** The preceding audit compared `atlas_feature_map.packet_id` only with `atlas_packets`, which missed the map’s documented owner. A read-only join now confirms all 1,992 non-null map IDs resolve to `nes_chrom_packets.packet_key`, and all 1,992 also match `source_ref` exactly. This is a valid map-to-NES projection linkage, not an Atlas canonical identity: 0 of those joined rows match `atlas_packets.packet_key`, 0 have `nes_chrom_packets.canonical IS TRUE`, 0 use a canonical-named identity lane, and 0 carry `lineage_version`. Keep this linkage for its existing NES owner, but do not use it to mint Atlas canonical identity or source/workspace admission. The prior “unresolved map identity” conclusion is superseded by this more precise classification.
- **2026-10-24 NCP-to-PacketKeyV2 alias census (read-only):** Checked the same 1,992 distinct NCP keys against `atlas_packet_identity_aliases`: zero alias rows, including zero `PACKET_KEY_V1_STORAGE_TO_V2` mappings, and zero canonical targets in `atlas_packets`. Therefore the existing `resolvePacketKeyResolutionV2` provider cannot resolve this NCP cohort; it correctly refuses legacy identities without an explicit V2 alias instead of deriving canonical identity from `source_ref`. Do not auto-create aliases from the exact source-ref matches: they establish projection correspondence only, not an approved canonical identity mapping, source/workspace revision, or actor authorization. The packet identity boundary remains open.

Missing grant provider refuses. A worktree-lease provider exists for service-agent task/run leases only, but is unregistered and does not authorize authenticated users. - **2026-10-24 OnT/EmbeddingGemma compatibility decision:** A 768-dimensional OnT model and EmbeddingGemma `semantic_768` are not interchangeable representations. OnT `encode()` uses its HierarchyTransformer backbone, while `encode_roles()` uses learned role rotations/scales; replacing the backbone without reinitializing/retraining those transforms and proving a compatible query/document recipe would invalidate the role geometry. Keep EmbeddingGemma as the existing canonical semantic embedding owner; any OnT/EmbeddingGemma experiment must be separately versioned and advisory, with its own vectors, frozen disjoint evaluation, and no in-place canonical reindex. Python orchestration is possible, but the inspected OnT OWL ingest depends on DeepOnto/JPype and Java; current Python 3.13 has no OnT/DeepOnto/JPype/Geoopt or Java, and CUDA is visible with OnT training auto-selecting CUDA. No installation, model load, inference, or training was run. The current goal's OWL/reasoner work is also blocked by absent authoritative relation axioms and no admitted repair example.
